國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國 10 月財政赤字大幅擴張、房市買氣探底，通膨壓力仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 美國 2027 財年首月財政赤字達 4,040.6 億美元，年增 42.1%。當月總收
入 3,689.4 億美元（年減 8.8%），總支出 7,730 億美元（年增 12.2%），
截至 10 月底公債總額升至 40.52 兆美元，財政壓力顯著加劇。 
⚫ 受房貸利率攀升至 7.33%影響，2026 年 10 月美國成屋銷售年率跌至 375
萬戶的 16 年新低。目前房價中位數微升至 41.67 萬美元，待售庫存增
至 159 萬戶，房市呈現「量縮、價微揚、庫存增」的態勢。考量目前就
業市場仍持續成長，一旦利率開始回落，預期將能有效釋放市場積壓的
買氣。 
⚫ 10 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，低於上月 0.6%，惟高於市場
預期；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%持平。住房、食品及能源價格續升，其中住
房貢獻逾三成，顯示通膨壓力仍存；惟受 2025 年 10 月政府停擺影響，
10 月年增率未發布。 
⚫ 10 月美國 PPI 月增 0.3%，較上月 1.4%明顯放緩，但年增率由 6.3%上
升至 6.5%，創 2022 年 11 月以來新高。能源與服務價格漲勢降溫，惟
食品、非能源商品及貨運價格續揚，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍高，後續須
關注成本轉嫁及對 Fed 利率政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 2026 年 APEC 峰會將於 11 月 18 日至 19 日在中國深圳舉行。會前中美
相繼延長部分關稅豁免及貿易限制暫停措施至 2027 年 1 月，中國暫停
24%對美加徵關稅，惟保留 10%稅率，美國則延長 178 項中國商品關稅
豁免，釋出經貿緩和訊號，有助降低供應鏈不確定性。惟雙方結構性分
歧仍存，後續須關注川習會談成果及貿易政策走向。 
⚫ 輝達創辦人黃仁勳因婚外情及涉國安爭議引發調查，於 11 月 12 日辭去
執行長及董事職務，結束 33 年掌舵生涯，由財務長克雷斯暫代執行長。
消息重挫輝達股價及市值，並引發市場對全球 AI 產業、供應鏈及公司
治理風險的高度關注。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 10 月財政赤字顯著擴大，反映歲入下滑及政府支出負擔加重；成
屋銷售創多年低點，顯示高利率持續壓抑房市需求。儘管 CPI 及 PPI 月
增幅放緩，惟核心通膨及生產端價格壓力仍頑強，顯示物價上行風險尚

經濟成長停滯，內外需動能轉弱 
經濟 ⚫ 2026 年第三季經濟成長率持平。 
短評 ⚫ 經濟成長動能趨緩，內外需表現均偏弱，惟企業投資回升提供支撐，後 續景氣走勢仍取決於民間消費復甦及外需改善程度。 
中 
國 
國際能源價格上漲抑制貿易順差擴張 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月出口年增率較 9 月續增 1.6 個百分點至 37.9%，出口總值為 4,123
億美元；進口年增率增 10.6 個百分點至 41.6%，進口總值為 3,071 億美
元。 
⚫ 10 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 9 月的 5%升至 5.7%；但消費者通膨(CPI)、
核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別回落 0.2 個百分點為 0.7%、0.8%。 
短評 
⚫ 由於國際能源價格上漲，進口增速高於出口，並抑制貿易順差擴張、減
緩外匯存底流入速度，惟此亦有助於減緩人民幣升值速度。 
⚫ 在民間消費乏力之下，企業仍大多自行吸收成本，加上政府控制零售燃
油及民生水電價格漲幅，故目前原油價格高漲僅少數反映於消費端。

115.11.16 
一、 美國—10 月財政赤字大幅擴張、房市買氣探底，通膨壓力仍具韌性 
2026 年 10 月(2027 財年第 1 個月)聯邦政府財政赤字達 4,040.6 億美元，較去
年同期的 2,843.3 億美元大幅擴大 42.1% 。當月政府總收入為 3,689.4 億美元，年
減 8.8%，主因個人所得稅淨收入由去年同期的 2,169.5 億美元下滑至 1,863.8 億
美元，且 10 月關稅退稅高達 67.3 億美元(遠高於去年同期的 17.4 億美元)，大幅
抵銷了 259.5 億美元的關稅總收入，致使關稅淨收入雖較 9 月的 59.1 億美元回升
至 192.2 億美元，仍較去年同期的 313.5 億美元大減 38.7%。支出方面，10 月政
府總支出達 7,730.0 億美元，年增 12.2%，除因 11 月 1 日適逢非營業日，導致部
分現役與退役軍人薪資、退伍軍人福利及醫療保險等款項提前至 10 月撥付外，
醫療保險(1,700.5 億美元)、社會安全(1,426.9 億美元)及國防(1,288.8 億美元)等主
要項目均較去年同期成長；同時，10 月聯邦淨利息支出攀升至 1,030.8 億美元，
年增 13.4%。整體而言，在歲入下滑與福利、國防及利息支出擴張的雙重影響下，
美國新財年開局赤字顯著走高，截至 10 月底聯邦公債總額已進一步推升至 40.52
兆美元(其中公眾持有債務為 32.74 兆美元)，龐大債務規模與後續利息負擔仍將
是牽動美國財政穩健度的核心變數。 
2026 年 10 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 375 萬戶，較上月減少
5.5%，較去年同期下滑 8.8%，創下自 2010 年 8 月以來的最低水準，主因是 30 年
期固定房貸平均利率攀升至 7.33%(高於上月的 6.86%及去年同期的 6.25%)，使購
屋負擔加重，全美住房可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 107.3 降至 100.7。觀察區域表
現，10 月西部、南部及中西部銷量分別月減 9.7%、6.5%及 3.2%，僅東北部維持
持平，且四大地區銷量均較去年同期衰退。儘管買氣受抑，全美成屋售價中位數
仍年增 0.4%至 41.67 萬美元，寫下連續 40 個月年增紀錄；供給端則持續擴增，
待售庫存升至 159 萬戶，月增 1.3%、年增 4.6%，相當於 5.1 個月供給量(高於上
月的 4.7 個月與去年同期的 4.4 個月)。全美房地產經紀人協會(NAR)首席經濟學
家 Lawrence Yun 指出，在高利率衝擊下，10 月成屋市場呈現「量縮、價微揚、
庫存增」的格局，但全國 8,800 萬名屋主整體財務狀況依然穩健，不良資產交易
僅占 2%，考量目前就業市場仍持續成長，未來一旦房貸利率回落，市場可望釋

出可觀的積壓買氣。 
2026 年 10 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，低於上月之
0.6%，惟高於市場預期的 0.3%；扣除食品與能源價格之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與
前月持平，並高於市場預期的 0.2%。在細項方面，住房指數月增 0.3%，貢獻整
體 CPI 月增幅逾三成，仍為推升通膨的重要因素；食品指數月增 0.5%，其中家庭
食品及外食價格分別上漲 0.4%及 0.5%；能源指數月增 0.7%，其中汽油價格及電
價分別上漲 1.0%及 0.9%，惟汽油漲幅較上月明顯放緩。核心項目方面，休閒娛
樂、醫療照護、汽車保險、家庭用品及教育等價格持續上揚，二手車及卡車價格
則下跌 1.2%，通訊價格亦小幅下降。另受 2025 年 10 月美國政府停擺影響，當月
未進行 CPI 調查並缺乏比較基期資料，美國勞工局未發布 2026 年 10 月整體 CPI
及多數主要指數之年增率。整體而言，10 月美國物價仍受住房、食品及能源價格
上漲帶動，核心 CPI 月增率則連續第三個月維持 0.3%，顯示服務類通膨壓力仍具
韌性，後續通膨走勢仍須持續觀察能源價格及住房成本變化。 
2026 年 10 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 0.3%，明顯低於上
月修正後之 1.4%，惟年增率由 6.3%升至 6.5%，創 2022 年 11 月以來最大漲幅，
顯示生產端價格壓力仍高。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 0.5%，低於上月之 2.5%，
逾六成漲幅來自不含食品及能源之商品價格上升，其中基本有機化學品價格上漲
3.9%；食品價格亦月增 1.3%，惟能源價格微降 0.1%，其中柴油價格下跌 7.9%。
另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.2%，低於上月之 1.0%，主要受運輸及倉儲
服務價格上漲 2.0%帶動，其中貨運卡車運輸價格上漲 3.9%，貢獻服務價格漲幅
逾八成，惟貿易服務價格下降 0.2%，抵銷部分漲幅。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務
後之核心最終需求 PPI 月增 0.5%，與上月持平，年增率則由 5.0%小幅降至 4.9%。
整體而言，10 月美國 PPI 月增幅明顯放緩，主要反映能源價格回落及服務價格漲
勢降溫，惟食品、部分非能源商品及貨運價格仍持續上漲，加上整體 PPI 年增率
進一步攀升，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍高，後續須關注企業成本轉嫁情形、能源及
運輸價格變化，以及對消費者物價與聯準會利率政策之影響。 
2026 年亞太經濟合作組織(APEC)領袖峰會將於 11 月 18 日至 19 日在中國深
圳舉行。在峰會召開前及「川習三會」前夕，中美雙方相繼延長部分關稅豁免與
暫停措施，釋出緩和貿易緊張關係的訊號，並為後續經貿協商創造較有利條件。
中國宣布自 11 月 10 日起續暫停實施 24%的對美加徵關稅兩個月，並保留 10%稅

率作為後續談判籌碼，同時延長對美船舶特別港務費、出口管制及部分反制措施
的暫停期限；美國則將 178 項中國商品的 301 關稅豁免延長至 2027 年 1 月 9 日，
涵蓋工業產品、太陽能設備及電子零組件等項目，並同步延後對中國船舶港口費
及岸橋設備加徵關稅措施。此番互釋善意反映雙方均有意維持經貿關係穩定，降
低企業經營及供應鏈不確定性，並為 APEC 期間的高層對話創造有利條件；惟中
方仍保留部分關稅措施，顯示未完全放棄反制工具，後續發展仍將視雙方談判進
展而定。 
輝達(NVIDIA)創辦人黃仁勳近日在被爆出與疑涉中國情報背景的女子黃樂
(Alice Huang)有婚外情並於 11 月 8 日坦承對妻子不忠致歉後，因外部與董事會調
查持續延燒，且《紐約時報》與《路透社》接連揭露其與直屬下屬輝達醫療保健
副總裁金伯莉·鮑爾(Kimberly Powell)及另一名內部女員工也存在婚外情，最終於
11 月 12 日正式宣布辭去總裁、執行長與董事會職位，結束其掌舵輝達 33 年的傳
奇生涯，並由財務長克雷斯(Colette Kress)暫代執行長兵符，同時任命哈維·瓊斯
(Harvey C. Jones)為首任董事會主席。同時，美國總統川普於 11 月 9 日證實聯邦
調查局(FBI)已介入調查該起事件及潛在的反情報國安疑慮，並表示雖與黃仁勳通
話且相信其不知女方背景的說法，仍會交由情報部門進一步核實。黃仁勳閃辭的
消息隨即震撼全球科技與金融市場，導致輝達股價在 11 月 12 日開盤後盤中一度
重挫逾 14%、單日市值一度蒸發約 6,000 億美元，不僅重創高「含輝量」台股 ETF
表現，更引發外界對全球 AI 產業版圖及美中科技供應鏈穩定性的高度憂慮。儘
管黃仁勳矽谷盟友與部分員工近期發起連署與遊說，試圖推動他重返輝達，但美
國財政部長貝森特(Scott Bessent)已公開呼籲輝達領導層應進行「大幅改組」，使
後續接班與公司治理動向備受矚目。 
二、歐元區—勞動市場開始出現疲態 
 歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率二次估計值維持 0.4%不變，顯示在地緣政治不確
定性升高、能源價格再度上揚的環境下，整體景氣仍具支撐力；今年前三季經濟
成長率(年化率)則大約為 1.1%，大致持平去年水準。從需求面來看，政府支出與
民間消費為主要成長動能，其中民間消費受惠於較高的儲蓄水準及穩定的就業市
場，減輕能源價格上漲的衝擊，並且電動車、油電混合車需求持續增加，抵銷利
率升高及地緣政治波動對投資帶來的負面影響。

### Page 8

用相關內容。

7

展望未來，從歐元區採購經理人指標(PMI)顯示第四季經濟仍可望延續溫和擴
張，在能源衝擊逐漸消退後，2027 年成長動能有望進一步增強。不過，第三季就
業呈現零成長，主要是德、法等核心國家就業疲弱，抵銷南歐國家就業增加的影
響，勞動市場可能逐漸走疲為未來復甦的隱憂。若就業狀況不如預期，恐進一步
抑制薪資表現，並削弱消費信心，進而影響經濟復甦。 
三、日本—經濟成長停滯，內外需動能轉弱 
 2026 年第三季經濟成長季增率持平，結束連續三季的經濟擴張，主要受通膨、
部分地區自然災害及全球需求疲弱影響，導致私人消費季減 0.3%，出口亦下滑
0.2%，加上進口反彈 2.5%，使淨貿易拖累 GDP 達 0.6 個百分點；惟企業投資季
增 1.6%，為近三季以來首次成長，對景氣形成一定支撐。整體而言，日本內外需
動能均有所轉弱，後續民間消費回溫及企業投資能否持續，將是觀察經濟能否恢
復成長的重要指標。 
四、中國—國際能源價格上漲抑制貿易順差擴張 
10 月出口年增率較 9 月續增 1.6 個百分點至 37.9%，出口總值為 4,123 億美
元；累計前十個月出口年增率上升為 22.7%，累積出口總值為 37,827 億美元。前
十個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(111.1%)、自動數據處理設備
(60.6%)、船舶(27.7%)、汽車(52.2%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備出口加速成長，
為 10 月出口年增率加速上升的主要因素。 
10 月進口年增率較 9 月增 10.6 個百分點至 41.6%，進口總值為 3,071 億美
元；累計前十個月進口值成長 29%至 27,401 億美元。該月原油、成品油進口金額
大幅增加，為帶動進口加速成長的主要因素；IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器
等)進口分別成長 65.1%、136.7%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器
關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前十個月貿易順差 10,426 億美
元，僅較去年同期增加 8.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原
物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴
國際供應鏈支持。此外，因貿易順差增幅有限，10 月外匯存底僅增 49 億美元至
約 3.41 兆美元。 
10 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 9 月的 5%升至 5.7%，主因原油價格居高不下，
燃料購進成本持續上漲，提高生產成本所致。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格

年增率續揚 10.5 個百分點至 35%，並帶動燃料加工業出廠價格年增率上揚至
29.9%，此外，對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學品、化纖出廠價格年增率加速升
至 16.4%、16%，顯示原油價格上漲的影響開始擴散至中下游產業。此外，由於
近期鋼鐵價格走弱，黑色金屬礦產開採出廠價格年減 6.4%，為主要拖累因素。 
10 月消費者通膨(CPI)、核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別回落 0.2 個百分點為
0.7%、0.8%。儘管燃油通膨續增 5.3 個百分點至 20.2%，但食品、房租、汽車、
旅遊價格漲幅皆有放緩，其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩，使消費者通膨較前月
下滑。整體而言，雖然企業生產成本持續上升，但在民間消費乏力之下，企業仍
大多自行吸收成本，加上政府控制零售燃油及民生水電價格漲幅，故目前原油價
格高漲僅少數反映於消費端。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔
或進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度通膨超標；印尼、菲律賓第三季 GDP 分別達 5.5%及
2.1% 
(1)印度：10 月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增率進一步上揚至 6.09%，遠高於 9 月
的 5.54%，其中食品通膨加速上升至 8.5%，反映聖嬰現象導致季風降雨量低於預
期，影響農作物收成，同時核心通膨率（剔除汽油、柴油和貴金屬）從 9 月的 3.2%
上升至 3.8%，顯示潛在通膨持續走強並有所擴大。由於通膨率高於預期且突破央
行 6%的目標區間上限，預期印度央行 12 月的政策會議將有進一步升息的壓力。 
(2)印尼：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 5.47%，私人消費成長 5.04%，主
要因政府採取措施，協助家庭抵禦通膨壓力和更高的借貸成本，惟受能源補貼負
擔加重導致財政空間收窄，政府支出大幅放緩至 8.95%，遠低於上季的 15.97%；
固定資產投資成長略放緩至 6.06%；另受石油和能源成本上漲影響，進口成長率
（7.31%）高於出口成長率（4.59%），致淨出口產生負貢獻，惟較上季對經濟的
拖累有所減緩。按產業別觀之，製造業(5.87%)、住宿業（11.22%）、運輸/倉儲業
（8.03%）和通訊業（7.13%）均有所成長，採礦業產出由負轉正 0.9%，抵銷農業
產出受聖嬰現象影響而大幅下降至 0.5%的影響。整體而言，第三季經濟成長優於
預期，惟在財政和貨幣政策收緊的背景下，國內需求可能進一步放緩。 
(3)菲律賓：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 2.1%，為 2009 年第四季以來（不
計新冠疫情影響）最疲軟的經濟成長，低於政府先前已下調的全年 3.5%至 4.5%

的目標，聖嬰現象令農業產量低迷和能源成本高漲持續抑制經濟活動。家庭消費
成長放緩至 2.4%，政府支出成長降至 6.8%，固定投資雖小幅復甦仍萎縮 4.4%，
所幸出口成長 8.6%、進口成長放緩至 7.7%，致淨出口呈正向貢獻。按產業別觀
之，工業成長加快至 5.4%，服務業小幅放緩至 9%，惟農業產出受氣候影響而衰
退 0.4%。整體而言，第三季經濟活動主要受氣候干擾，農業、服務產出下滑並影
響消費支出，固定投資在公共建設投資帶動下有好轉跡象，然而央行升息提高借
貸成本，對私人投資產構成壓力，隨極端天氣事件的影響逐漸消退、家庭在年底
假期前消費意願提升、以及政府加快基礎設施建設，將有助於第四季經濟加快復
甦。 
六、台灣—AI 商機持續發酵，出口動能強勁 
10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，年增 58.9%，創歷史新高，主要由 AI 及高效能運
算需求帶動，其中資通與視聽產品、電子零組件合計約占總出口 83%，顯示 AI 伺
服器、半導體及相關零組件已成為出口成長核心。同時，對美出口達 329.3 億美
元、年增 55.9%，東協亦大幅成長 83.8%，反映 AI 需求及全球供應鏈重組正帶動
出口市場結構改變。惟進口年增 75.1%，增幅高於出口，後續宜觀察進口增加是
否有效轉化為產能及出口，以及 AI 需求、高基期與全球科技業資本支出變化對
出口動能的影響。

Earnings revision: 
We revise up our 2026 earnings estimates by 6% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
down our 2026E revenue estimates by 1%). We revise up 2028E earnings estimates by 
1% to reflect a better product mix with higher contribution from the server business. We 
now expect the GM expansion to be faster due to the higher server business 
contribution, and thus revise up GPM by 1.2/0.3/0.4ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation: 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AVC with a new 12m TP of NT$4,820 (from NT$4,736), 
based on 30.6x 2027E P/E (which is above AVC’s historical average +1 STDV. forward 
trading P/E, reflecting our positive view on AVC on the rising penetration rate of 
liquid-cooled AI servers), and factor in our latest earnings revision. 
Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

AVC is a global leading supplier of thermal solutions on Server, PC, Telecom and 
consumer electronics. We expect the company’s cooling revenue contributions to grow 
to 66%/71%/74% in 2026/27/28E, driven by: (1) rising computing power of AI servers 
driving liquid cooling demand, (2) capacity ramp-up in Vietnam of 3D Vapor Chambers 
for GPUs and liquid cooling components, and (3) AI PC’s launch in 2025, featuring more 
powerful CPU/ NPU/ Memory. We are Buy rated. Our target price is based on 2027E P/E 
of 30.6x which is higher than its past 5-yr avg.+1stv. of 11x, reflecting the product mix 
upgrade toward servers cooling / liquid cooling with stronger earnings growth and 
better margins, which we believe supports the higher valuation. 
We are Buy rated on AVC with a 12m TP of NT$4,820, based on 30.6 2027E P/E, which is

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

MPI Corp. (6223.TWO): Richer MEMS mix to drive GM expansion ahead; 
3Q26 beat; TP up to NT$8,600; Buy
12 November 2026 | 12:27AM HKT
Oct revenues on track: Oct revenues came in at NT$2,232mn, +93% YoY and +9% 
MoM (vs. 3Q26 revenues +13% QoQ), accounting for 33% of our 4Q26E revenue 
forecast. Looking into 4Q26, we expect revenue to grow 13% QoQ on continued 
AI-driven demand for both VPC and MEMS probe cards and initial shipments to the 
US CPU customer, and we raise our GM assumption to 62.4% (vs. 60.4% previously) 
to reflect the richer VPC/MEMS mix seen in 3Q26. For 2027, we expect MEMS 
capacity additions, share gains at the US CPU customer and rising pin count per card 
to drive growth, with GM expanding further as MEMS mix rises. 3Q26 beat: Revenue 
was pre-announced. GM of 61.7% came in above GSe/Bloomberg consensus of 
59.8%/59.5%, which we attribute to higher VPC/MEMS shipments for AI-related 
applications. Together with a lower opex ratio of 23.1% (vs. GSe/cons. of 
23.6%/24.1%) on larger revenue scale, operating income grew 26% QoQ, 8.5% / 
11.5% ahead of GSe/cons. EPS of NT$19.18 was 8.1% / 11.0% above GSe/cons. With 
higher GM, we raise our 2026-28E EPS by 5%/3%/1%, and raise our 12m TP to 
NT$8,600 (implied 53x 2027E P/E vs. 90% NI YoY of 2027-28E on avg.). Maintain Buy.

MEMS capacity ramp and new customers to drive growth: Our 4Q26E forecast 
implies average monthly revenue of NT$2,226mn in November-December (flat vs. 
October). On the margin front, we expect product mix to remain favorable for GM, as 
VPC/MEMS capacity additions and new customer ramps continue. Longer term, we 
remain positive on MPI’s growth outlook, driven by 1) MEMS and VPC capacity 
expansion, where we keep our capacity assumption of 2.5mn/8.0mn pins per month 
for VPC/MEMS by end-2027E, which we see as well supported as multiple AI ASIC

and networking programs move into volume; 2) share gains at the US CPU customer,

Earnings revisions: We incorporate the 3Q26 results into our model and revise our 
2026/2027/2028E EPS by +4.8%/+3.2%/+1.2%, as we factor in 1) higher GM, reflecting 
the 3Q26 results and a richer VPC/MEMS mix, and 2) higher revenue 
(+0.8%/+1.2%/+1.5% vs. our prior forecasts). Overall, we now model revenue growth of 
63%/106%/47% YoY over 2026-28E (vs. 61%/105%/46% previously), with GM 
expanding to 60.7%/63.1%/63.9% (vs. 59.6%/62.2%/63.7% previously).

Valuation: As a result of our earnings revisions, we raise our 12-month TP to NT$8,600 
(from NT$8,500). Our 12-month TP is still based on a target P/E of 40x (1.7x of 3-year 
average forward P/E) applied to our 2028E EPS, and discounted back to 2027E at 12.4% 
CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.3x beta (sourcing Bloomberg), (2) a

Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology – MPI (6223.TWO) 
Valuation: We are Buy rated on MPI. Our 12-month TP of NT$8,600 is based on a target 
P/E of 40x (1.7x of 3-year average forward P/E) applied to our 2028E EPS, and 
discounted back to 2027E at 12.4% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.3x 
beta (sourcing Bloomberg), (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 
6.25% (in line with GS house view). 
Key risks to our views: 1) softer AI/HPC demand, 2) slower penetration into new TAM, 
and 3) intensifying competition.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

Document 174: 261117_daiwa_merida

### Page 1

Summary: Merida hosted its 3Q26 investor call on 17 November after market hours, 
following release of its 3Q26 results on 11 November (see our memo). Merida’s gross 
margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our estimate 
(16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Factoring in the equity investment loss of 
TWD150m+, its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the 
consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results recap. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales, the price discount 
for further inventory digestion and the depreciation of the Euro. Factoring in the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus estimates by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was 
TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).  
• 2026 outlook update. Merida revised down its 2026 full-year shipments to 960k units 
(Taiwan plant 280k, down from previous 340k; China plant 680k, down from previous 
700k), as it continues to manage its existing model shipments and expects new models 
in 2027 to bring stronger demand. The company now sees recovery in Europe and 
China, with inventory digestion coming to an end: 9M26 sales in Europe grew 14% YoY, 
driven by strong demand for road bikes and gravel bikes; 9M 26 sales in China grew by 
1% while shipments grew by 7% (vs. revenue guidance of declining 15-20% at the 
beginning of 2026), driven by low- and mid-end models. 
• SBC expected to be profitable in 4Q26. Due to SBC’s conservative tax assumption 
(over 100% effective tax rate) in 1Q-3Q26, if and when the tax rate realised at year-end 
is determined to be less than currently allocated for, we expect there will be a huge profit 
recognised in 4Q26 to offset the estimated after-tax loss recognised in 1Q-3Q26. Merida 
and SBC will review the tax assumption policy after full-year results are out, as the 
policy has caused a great deal of investor confusion. Including USD15m of tariff refund 
in 3Q26, SBC’s pre-tax profit grew by 42% YoY for 9M26. Merida expects there is 
another USD5m of tariff refund to collect in 4Q26.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 
13.2x/10.6x, based on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-5-year one-year-forward PER 
trading range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the 
EU and the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory 
digestion.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

### Page 1

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H)

ABF substrate & CCL: making the case for 
limited downside after valuation reset

Price Objective Change

Valuation implies sizable 2028 EPS downside   
Broadly speaking, share prices of the ABF substrate and CCL stocks under our Greater 
China coverage fell by 40-50% from their October peaks to their November lows. Based 
on the two scenarios presented in this report, we believe current valuations may imply 
the market’s over-concerns about the inflection of AI server cycle or a slowdown in 
capex growth. Using closing price as of Nov. 16th, the stocks traded at 7-13x 2028E P/E 
under scenario 1, in which we assume a 15% shortfall in AI-related revenue vs. our 
estimates (Exhibit 3). These multiples are below the midpoint of their valuation range 
before the rise of AI server demand from mid-2023. Under scenario 2, we divide the 
current share price by our long-term anchored P/E multiple to calculate the 2028E EPS 
imlied by the share price. Based on our analysis, current valuations imply a 40-90% 
shortfall (or discount) in AI-related revenue versus our estimates (Exhibit 4). 
Raise estimates but lower P/E multiples  
For 2027-28E OP, we raise our estimates by 4-9%/19-31%/3-6%/19-24%/22-23%/32-
33% for Unimicron/Kinsus/NYPCB/EMC/TUC/Shengyi, reflecting a general beat in 3Q26 
GM and ongoing price hike outlook across ABF/BT substrate and CCL. However, we 
lower our POs on these names (Exhibit 1) as we lower P/E multiples by ~30% to reflect 
sector-wide de-rating in AI server supply chain, while the valuation period is unchanged. 
See Exhibit 10 for details of the changes in valuation multiples.  
Stay largely positive on ABF substrate and CCL sectors 
Despite lowered POs and multiples, our positive stance on the ABF substrate sector is 
unchanged, reflecting our expectation of a more sustained supply deficit through 2028 
as well as the T-glass price hike by Nittobo (see report) and Resonac, which should set 
the stage for substrate makers to lift ASP further. We thus reiterate Buy on Unimicron, 
Kinsus and NYPCB. As for CCL, we are also generally positive on the S/D outlook given 
the lead time of certain equipment is still at least 1.5-2 years, yet at single stock level 
we are more constructive on Buy-rated names including 1) EMC with at least 70-80% 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above in 2027-28 and its estimated 50-60% share of 
the AI-related high-end CCL market, along with potential exposure to M10 adoption in 
SpaceX’s AI server rack based on our channel checks; and 2) TUC being one of the few 
high-end CCL suppliers with more aggressive capacity expansion during 2026-30 (~3x 
over the period). We also see potential for additional ~2mn sheet/month capacity 
addition in 2030 (in Thailand).   For discussions on ABF substrate S/D model update and 
our thoughts on CPO impact, see page 2.

### Page 2

ABF substrate – wider undersupply expected vs previously  
We update ABF substrate industry S/D model with this report, and we now expect 
7%/18%/20% under-supply (was 7%/14%/19%) in 2026/27/28 thanks to the raised TAM 
(see report, Core Wars: raising CPU TAM to $240bn, still early stages of agents) for AI 
accelerators and CPUs (central processing units), which leads us to estimate 36%/24% 
(was 32%/26%) demand growth by the industry in 2027/28. After reflecting the demand 
updates, AI accelerators are expected to account for 41%/52%/55% (was 
40%/46%/51%) of demand in 2026/27/28. Overall, our industry S/D model also 
considers the 2.5x SAP (semi additive process) demand growth across 2026-30 shared 
by Ibiden, which incorporates 1.5x and 4.4x demand growth by server CPU and AI 
accelerators respectively over the same period.  
Thoughts on CPO impact for CCL/PCB 
Our checks with supply chain suggest that many of the CCL materials are upgraded to 
M9-grade (based on low Dk2 cloth and hyper very low profile 4 copper foil), when 
pluggable optical modules are upgraded to OBO (on-board optics) and/or NPO (near 
package optics). In terms of CCL-grade vs PCB layer count, there is a general 
categorization in AI server and adjacent applications shown in Exhibit 12, and it indicates 
that M8-grade or above CCL will be required when the board layer count moves up to 30 
or higher. Further, given that CPO gen-2 with OOI (optics on interposer) is unlikely to 
ramp before 2028, alongside the upgrade in transmission to 3.2T, CCL material used in 
mainboard may have already been upgraded to M10-grade by then under a non- 
CPO/OOI architecture. This means CPO/OOI adoption may only result in a material 
downgrade to M8/M9-grade.

Price objective basis & risk 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

### Page 13

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,450 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 36% and 25% in 2027-28E (vs 8% and 10% in 
2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development. 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,650 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1 standard deviation 
above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, 
as we expect Unimicron's earnings to be supported by its high-end technology offerings, 
where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating) 
Ratings dispersion guidelines for coverage clusterR2 
Buy ≥ 10% ≤ 70% 
Neutral ≥ 0% ≤ 30% 
Underperform N/A ≥ 20% 
R2Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster. 
INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be secure) 
and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, sector, 
region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

Feedback: Read-across from
Three-Circle's comments for
Yageo
Positive pricing signals for Yageo: Chaozhou Three-Circle (300408.SZ/6951.HK,
not covered) spoke at our Asia Pacific Summit. It said that its 3Q26 MLCC ASPs rose
20–30% q/q on catch-up increases in mid/low-capacitance products. We see this as

supportive of firmer standard MLCC pricing at Yageo.
Tightness extends into 1H27: Three-Circle said its utilization rate was at 100% and
it expects high-capacitance shortages through 1H27. Management said Murata/
SEMCO’s capacity shifts were happening and this would continue to tighten
standard MLCC supply, supporting our view on Yageo (rated Overweight) and its
utilization and pricing.
Technology limits competitive overlap: Three-Circle said its  AI mix was single-digit,

with small, high-capacitance MLCCs still a capability gap. This suggests limited
immediate competition for the more demanding products within Yageo’s high-
capacitance portfolio.
Three-Circle said its >40% CY26 capacity growth outlook implies >70B MLCC
units/month by year-end, versus ~50B at the start of the year. This compares to
our estimate on Yageo at ~80B/month, although we don't know the exact
specifications used by either company, so that could limit direct capacity

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

4576 大銀微(HMC)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研 究 員：洪 國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
 在半導體先進封裝、AI 及矽光子等高階應用需求暢旺，其中
微米與奈米級定位系統交期 5~6 個月，精密運動及控制元件交
期約 3~4 個月，呈現供不應求。 
 受惠於晶圓大廠持續擴充先進製程、後段設備廠訂單持續成
長，大銀微在超高精度平台訂單已經看到 2027 年年中，另外
搭配新產能將於 2Q27 投產。預估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位
系統營收金額 31.5 億元，YoY+28.0%。 
一. 公告 3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.14 元 
大銀微 3Q26 營收金額 12.18 億元，YoY+76.73%，QoQ+19.01%，
營業利益 2.96 億元，YoY+312.6%，QoQ+61.12%，稅後盈餘 2.57
億元，YoY+255.8%，QoQ+123.72%，稅後 EPS 2.14 元。 
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.44 元 
大銀微 10/2026 營收金額 4.10 億元，YoY+68.75%，MoM-3.96%。
在訂單呈現滿載水準，預估大銀微 4Q26 營收金額 12.84 億元，
YoY+69.94% ， QoQ+5.37% ，預估營業利益 3.32 億元，
YoY+232.13% ， QoQ+12.21% ，預估稅後盈餘 2.92 億元，
YoY+223.25%，QoQ+13.78%，預估稅後 EPS 2.44 元。 
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 6.50 元 
大銀微 2026 年在半導體、PCB 與矽光子檢測裝置需求延續，帶
動微米奈米級高精度定位系統訂單維持數個月的能見度。另外中
國大陸工具機谷底復甦，推升控制元件產品需求，帶動大銀微 2026
年營運成長。預估大銀微 2026 年營收 43.8 億元，YoY+61.37%，
預估稅後盈餘 7.79 億元，YoY+222.63%，預估稅後 EPS 6.50 元。 
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 9.00 元 
大銀微 2027 年在推動的 One HIWIN Solution 單一零組件供應
商，往次系統、完整定位平台以及共同開發解決方案的角色移動，
搭配同集團上銀資源整合，將有助於大銀微產品附加價值提升。
宏遠預估大銀微 2027 年營收金額 53.00 億元，YoY+21.00%，預
估稅後盈餘 10.79 億元，YoY+38.52%，預估稅後 EPS 9.00 元。 
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 252 元 
觀察國內傳動元件同業 2027 年營收成長率亞德客 ROE 21%，營
收成長率 10%，PER 15X ；上銀 ROE 11% ，營收成長率 16%，
PER 22X，大銀微 ROE 21%，營收成長率 21%，PER 21.6X，以
ROE 與營收成長率相較同業來的佳，評價與同業相較仍在低檔水
準，故投資建議買進，目標價 252 元（28X 2027 年稅後 EPS）

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
大銀微系統股份有限公司創立於 1997 年 4 月，資本額 11.98 億
元，並於 2019 年 9 月正式掛牌上市。大銀微行屬於上銀集團旗
下的核心關係企業。大銀微聚焦精準定位與高性能驅動控制技
術，並以機電整合、產品製造及技術服務之整體能力作為核心競
爭優勢，深耕半導體、電子生產設備、智慧自動化及工具機等領
域。受惠半導體先進製程與封裝需求成長，大銀微以領先業界之
精密定位技術及系統整合方案擴大服務範圍，針對客戶需求提供
專業精密定位系統整合方案，透過技術互補與研發合作，協助全 
球頂尖半導體客戶縮短開發週期並提升產出效益。

二. 2026 年營運概況及 2027 年展望 
大銀行營收主要有精密運動及控制元件、微米與奈米級定位系統
與其他三部分。

預估 2027 年精密運動及控制元件營收金額 21.00 億元，
YoY+11.7%

精密運動及控制元件產品線包括：線性馬達、直驅馬達、真空馬
達、力矩馬達、伺服馬達、高速主軸馬達、位置量測系統等，並
搭配自行開發的驅動器及控制器，達奈米級高精度定位控制功
能。廣泛應用於半導體設備/自動化搬送/FPD 設備/PCB 設備
/AOI 檢測設備/SMT 設備/雷射加工器/各式機器手臂/工具機/電
動車相關/太陽能等不同產業。精密運動及控制元件 2025 年營收
金額 12.64 億元，YoY+3.1%。

精密運動及控制元件 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 13.83 億元，
YoY+45.43%。在中國大陸對驅動器需求高，目前處於供不應求狀
態，帶動包括線性馬達、直驅馬達、伺服馬達產能稼動率在高檔
水準，預估 2026 年精密運動及控制元件產品營收金額 18.80 億
元，YoY+48.7%。

預期 2027 年，在中國大陸工具機出口額與金屬切削設備產業仍呈
現正向發展，整體需求大於供給，訂單能見度己達 3~4 個月，預
估 2027 年精密運動及控制元件產品營收金額 21.00 億元，
YoY+11.7%。

YoY+69.5%。在 AI 產業熱潮帶動半導體產業投資本支出持續加
速，間接推升微米與奈米級定位系統需求，其中半導體定位平台
能見度約 5~6 個月，超高精度平台甚至可看到 2027 年中，在此
熱潮帶動下，預估 2026 年微米與奈米級定位系統營收金額 24.60
億元，YoY+72.4%。

預期 2027 年，受惠於晶圓大廠持續擴充先進製程、後段設備廠訂
單持續成長，大銀微在超高精度平台訂單已經看到 2027 年年中，
包括 CoWoP、CoPoS、FoPLP 都持續配合客戶打樣中，另外搭
配新產能將於 2Q27 投 產。預 估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位系統營
收金額 31.5 億元，YoY+28.0%。

圖四、在半導體製程演進，持續往高階設備升級，提升平台方案價值

資料來源：公司&宏遠投顧彙整

稅後 EPS 6.50 元。

預估 2027 年稅後盈餘 10.79 億元，YoY+38.52%，稅 後 EPS 9.00
元

大銀微 2027 年在推動的 One HIWIN Solution 單一零組件供應
商，往次系統、完整定位平台以及共同開發解決方案的角色移動，
搭配同集團上銀資源整合，將有助於大銀微產品附加價值提升。

估營業利益 13.86 億元，YoY+45.4%，預估稅後盈餘 10.79 億元，
YoY+38.52%，預估稅後 EPS 9.00 元。

Document 243: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G는 +11.5%, ASP는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

SK하이닉스 (000660)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking
the cash rate after the November increase. Since then, our
conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we
will publish the November Market Outlook with revised
forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing

market have all been revised down in 2027.
Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for
calendar 2027 more decisively inside the RBA’s 2–3% target
range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment
rate forecast has been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on
this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash rate from
here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.

A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The
housing market has undershot our forecasts over the past
couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been
coming in under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan
inquiries have taken another leg down in October after the
September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior
months, with the additional impact of the November rate hike
still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer.

Consumer income and spending have so far been quite
resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest
rates. But our Westpac Card Tracker is starting to show that
discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite
weak. We would not over-emphasise this point, but it does
imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB)

meeting, business survey data has shown weaker conditions
and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show
further evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-
labour cost shock that is not being fully passed through to
consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil
and fuel prices are already materially lower than the track
assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-
fourth lower than on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts

were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of
fuel costs to other prices, as well as easing consumer inflation
perceptions and expectations.
An RBA peak of greater conviction
Forecast changes
• A full update to our forecasts has been published in our
November Market Outlook.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
In the media conference following the November meeting,
Governor Bullock flagged that it was a good time to “take

stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the
market read this as a signal that the MPB would pause at the
December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to
downside risks than the internal members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is
looking even less likely given signs in the data and surveys.
The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and

senior officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a
downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though,
a rate hike in 2027 would require either a material new
inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would
constitute a policy error on the part of the RBA (something
that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the talents
and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with

the dissenting voters in the MPB that the downside risks need
more attention.
Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to
February, we think there will be more evidence that the
domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation
outlook with it.
“A deeper housing downturn and early signs of
cracks in the consumer sector point to an even
softer 2027."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the
passthrough of energy and technology input inflation. His
conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the monthly

rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward
supporting an increase in our policy rate of 25 basis points
at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI
inflation data out later today are similar to the past two
monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent
or even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

Document 330: Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

Related articles
this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

投資焦點
► 今年9月適逢地緣擾動持續推升高油價、聯準
會重啟 升息 、財政部與企 業發債 浪潮 等影
響，形塑債市今年以來單月最差表現。檢視
2021年以來美國投資級債前十大單月最差總
報酬，2022年聯準會3月啟動升息年度就佔
一半以上，顯示利率風險仍為近5年來債市最
大風險，然檢視單月最差報酬表現未來期間
總報酬，除2022年1月和3月聯準會重啟升息
前夕與當月表現明顯較弱，多數於未來1年後
多呈現正報酬，反映隨利率走升放大孳息效
益，同時挹注債券長期持有之報酬空間。
► 今年來債券市場利率波動明顯推升信用債利
差，其 中僅 以低 信 評債利 差升幅 較顯 ，此
外，9月以來利率連動度高及AI相關產業債種
也成為利差擴大重災區，凸顯債市投資人減
輕市場不確定性的投資傾向，考量信用債市
歷經利率上行利空洗禮，信用利差同步擴大

創造收益空間，可把握債信體質仍佳企業債
鎖利良機，並透過時間複利力量強化收益。
利率波動升，僅低信評利差擴大程度相對明顯9月為美國投資級債今年最差單月報酬
用時間換空間，單月最差表現形塑未來報酬機會

### Page 8

8
► 受聯準會重啟升息影響，過去一年美國公債
殖利率曲線持續陡峭化，其中利率最大升幅
落在短天期的2至3年期間，美國核心通膨與
就業仍具韌性，聯準會將持續保持緊縮貨幣
政策步調來應對高通膨，考量美國高物價與
高利率環境短期難消，宜透過縮短債券配置
天期並進行動態調整，為因應後續利率不確
定性環境的良策。
► 為因應利率不確定性，投資人可採取債券梯
的投資配置，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，債券梯投資重點主要在
於，根據殖利率曲線型態及利率動態建立不
同期間債券梯，假設市場利率走升且殖利率
曲線正斜率，建立短天期債券梯除了降低利
率風險，有 短期 閒 置資金 及孳息 需求 投資
人，亦可取得穩定孳息收益。若後續投資人
有短期資金需求，可以視情況將短期債券到
期後資金回收，降低只投資單一債券須持至
到期或直接變現的流動性風險。
投資焦點
美國公債殖利率曲線持續為正斜率
高利率波動持續，用策略迎戰市場擾動
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理
建立短天期債券梯，分散風險及穩定現金流

債券梯
經過第2年第一檔債券到期，視資金需求及市況與利率動
態決定後續配置

轉投資股市
短天期債券梯：假設買入2檔各3、4、5及7年到期債券，
聚焦短期穩定收益
► 債券梯是一種分散投資方式，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，可兼具：
1) 分散利率風險：將資金分別買進不同到期年期的債券，以分
散利率風險。
2) 分散信用風險：分散投資不同發行機構債券，不會重壓單一
債券並可分散信用違約風險。
3) 降低流動性風險：若投資人有短期資金需求，可以視情況將
短期債券到期後資金回收，降低只投資單一債券須持至到期
或直接變現之流動性風險。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長，市場靜待財報結果與展望，和對AI資本支出回收影響。
考量地緣衝突、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉，短期金融市場紛擾不止，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變，逢股市拉回時可分批布局，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點，全球利率走揚有利銀行股。此外，地緣衝突頻傳，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間，殖利率偏高環境使中短天期債券提供
吸引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後
利差具吸引力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。

 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高，使金價短期承壓，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 防禦為上築防線，擾動休止再回成長

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, declined to –0.14% in October
from +0.06% in September.

The Leading Index weakened again in October after steadier
results through August-September. At –0.14% the latest six
month growth rate is back in negative, below-trend territory,
broadly similar to the soft reads around the start of the year.
The shift points to a return to below-trend growth momentum
in the first half of 2027. Westpac expects annual GDP growth
to slow back to 1.4% for calendar 2026, with a further
moderation to a sluggish 1%yr pace in 2027.

The Leading Index growth rate is still a touch firmer than
six months ago, the current pace of –0.14% comparing to a
–0.2% back in April, when the War in Iran was first impacting.
The component detail shows mixed moves over the
intervening period. Four components have started to exert
a bigger drag: total hours worked taking –0.2ppts off the
headline growth rate; with a further 0.15ppt drag coming
from the combined effect of softer commodity prices

(measured in AUD terms); a narrowing yield spread (mainly
reflecting the RBA tightening pushing up short term rates);
and rising job loss fears captured by the Westpac-Melbourne
Institute Unemployment Expectations Index. This has been
more than offset by a reduced (but still negative) drag from
the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Expectations
Index (adding +0.25ppts); and firmer reads on local equity
markets and dwelling approvals (adding +0.17ppts on a

combined basis.
Overall, the component mix shows labour market conditions
and tightening monetary policy have become more prominent
drags on momentum. While the Leading Index signal is not
overly weak the decline is clearly worth monitoring with the
detail suggesting there may be more weakening to come.
Leading Index moves back below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines

variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken
individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

Document 407: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-19)
